\chapter{The one-dimensional area law and matrix product state approximation}
\label{ch:one_dimensional_chain}

% The chain specialization of the 2026 area-law and PEPS manuscripts, following the
% TNLean note docs/notes/one_dimensional_note. Every constant is explicit. The model is
% stated natively on the chain of L sites. The chain proof uses only the site-generic
% layer TNLean/Lattice and QICLean; no module of TNLean/MPS/AreaLaw imports TNLean.PEPS.

The chain of $L$ sites carries a local dimension $q$, a finite-range Hamiltonian
with one Hermitian term of norm at most $J$ per support of diameter at most $R$,
and a unit ground vector separated from the rest of the spectrum by a gap
$\Delta$. On a chain the two-dimensional argument of \cite{OpenAI2026AreaLaw}
collapses to three steps: the marginal tail bound at an interval cut, the
buffered discount with a buffer of constant width, and the Araki--Lieb
inequality. The matrix product state approximation of
\cite{OpenAI2026PolynomialPEPS} then follows from the tail bound alone by
truncating the Schmidt decompositions across the cuts in sequence. This chapter
follows \cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}; all constants are explicit, and the
area-law constant is linear in $1+J/\Delta$. The two finite-dimensional cores,
the tail bound and the buffered discount, are stated in the companion library
for an arbitrary finite set of sites and are instantiated here directly.

\section{Chains, supports and Hamiltonians}

\begin{definition}[Chains, intervals and finite-range Hamiltonians]
    \label{def:chain_model}
    \lean{
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.interval,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.mem_interval,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.interval_mono,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.interval_eq_empty,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.interval_zero_eq_univ,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_interval_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.IsChainSupport,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainSupport,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.IsChainSupport.card_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.supportDim_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.one_le_supportDim,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.operator,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.operator_isHermitian,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.zero,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.cutLogBudget,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.one_le_cutLogBudget
    }
    \leanok
    The \emph{chain of $L$ sites} has sites $0,\ldots,L-1$, each carrying
    $\mathbb C^q$. For natural numbers $a\le b$ the \emph{interval} $[a,b)$ is
    the set of sites with position at least $a$ and less than $b$; it has at
    most $b-a$ sites, and its dilation $[a-d,b+d)$ is clipped at the ends of the
    chain. A \emph{support of range $R$} is a nonempty set of sites of diameter
    at most $R$; it has at most $R+1$ sites and Hilbert-space dimension at most
    $q^{R+1}$. A \emph{finite-range Hamiltonian} assigns to every support $X$
    of range $R$ a Hermitian term $h_X$ acting as the identity off $X$ with
    operator norm at most $J$, zero terms allowed, and
    $H=\sum_X h_X$. A \emph{gapped ground vector} with gap $\Delta$ is a unit
    vector $\Omega$ with $H\Omega=E_0\Omega$ and
    $H-E_0\ge\Delta(1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|)$. For a cut $A$ the
    \emph{cut budget} is
    $\mathcal B_A=1+\sum_X\log^2(e\,q^{|X|})$ over the supports $X$ meeting
    both $A$ and its complement; it is at least one.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[A bond sum separated by two windows]
    \label{thm:chain_two_window_sum}
    \lean{Finset.sum_range_eq_five_parts_of_two_windows,
        Fin.sum_univ_eq_five_parts_of_two_windows}
    \leanok
    Let $M$ be an abelian monoid written additively, and let
    $a,b,\ell,N$ be natural numbers with $a+2\ell\le b$ and
    $b+2\ell\le N$. For a sequence $f:\mathbb N\to M$,
    \begin{align}
        \sum_{t<N}f(t)
         & =\sum_{t<a}f(t)+\sum_{r<2\ell}f(a+r)\notag   \\
         & \quad+\sum_{r<b-(a+2\ell)}f(a+2\ell+r)\notag \\
         & \quad+\sum_{r<2\ell}f(b+r)
        +\sum_{r<N-(b+2\ell)}f(b+2\ell+r).
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_sum}
    \end{align}
    The same identity holds when $f$ is given only on
    $\{0,\ldots,N-1\}$: every shifted index in~(\ref{eq:chain_two_window_sum})
    belongs to this set. Empty windows and empty complementary intervals
    are permitted. Applied to a bond array, this is the additive grouping
    used in~\cite[Section~4.1]{Arad2016RigorousRG}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    Split the sum successively at $b+2\ell$, $b$, $a+2\ell$ and $a$.
    The inequalities on the endpoints give the complementary lengths
    in~(\ref{eq:chain_two_window_sum}). For a finite array, extend $f$ by zero
    outside its domain and reindex each interval by its finite length.
    Each shifted index is less than $N$, so the extension agrees with $f$
    in every summand.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The number of range-one supports]
    \label{thm:chain_range_one_support_count}
    \lean{
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_chainSupport_range_one_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.norm_operator_le_of_range_one
    }
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    A chain of $L$ sites has at most $2L$ supports of range one.
    Consequently, a range-one Hamiltonian whose terms have norm at most
    $J$ satisfies $\|H\|\le2LJ$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    A support of range one is a singleton or a pair of adjacent sites.
    It is therefore determined by its least site and its cardinality,
    which is either one or two. This gives the bound $2L$.
    If $L>0$, the norm bound on a singleton term implies $J\ge0$, and the
    triangle inequality gives $\|H\|\le\sum_X\|h_X\|\le2LJ$.
    If $L=0$, there are no interaction terms and both sides vanish.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Energy from ground-state overlap for a range-one chain]
    \label{thm:chain_range_one_overlap_energy}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.re_inner_sub_le_of_overlap_of_range_one,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.re_inner_le_of_overlap_div_of_range_one}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $H$ be a range-one Hamiltonian on a chain of $L$ sites, with each
    interaction term of norm at most one. Let $E_0,\Delta$ be real and let
    $\Omega$ be a unit vector satisfying $H\Omega=E_0\Omega$ and
    $H-E_0\ge\Delta(1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|)$.
    If $v$ is a unit vector with $|\langle\Omega,v\rangle|\ge1-\delta$, then
    \begin{align}
        \operatorname{Re}\langle v,Hv\rangle-E_0
         & \le8L\delta.
        \label{eq:chain_range_one_overlap_energy}
    \end{align}
    In particular, when $L>0$, overlap at least $1-c/L$ gives energy at
    most $E_0+8c$.

    This is a general norm estimate supporting the first energy inference
    in Lemma~l:within of~\cite[lines 509--515]{Landau2015Polynomial}.
    The source assumes positive adjacent-pair terms; here singleton and
    adjacent-pair terms need only be Hermitian. The correction for
    unsquared overlap is recorded in \cite{gap:lvv13_witness_energy}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_range_one_support_count}
    The support count gives $\|H\|\le2L$. The eigenvector equation and
    $\|\Omega\|=1$ give $|E_0|\le\|H\|$, hence
    $\|H-E_0\,1\|\le\|H\|+|E_0|\le4L$.
    The energy estimate from overlap with an eigenvector in
    \cite[``Energy bound from overlap with an eigenvector'']{QICLean2026Blueprint}
    now gives (\ref{eq:chain_range_one_overlap_energy}). Substituting
    $\delta=c/L$ proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Nearest-neighbour supports at two cuts]
    \label{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_supports}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.interval_edge_classification_of_lt}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $L,u,v$ be natural numbers with $u<v$ and $v+1<L$.
    Place the cuts immediately after sites $u$ and $v$, as in
    \cite{Arad2016RigorousRG}. Set $T=\{0,\ldots,u\}$, $E=\{u+1,\ldots,v\}$
    and $U=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}\setminus(T\cup E)$.
    For an edge $r$ with $r+1<L$, write $D_r=\{r,r+1\}$.
    Then $D_u\subseteq T\cup E$, $D_u\cap T=\{u\}$,
    $D_v\cap T=\varnothing$ and $D_v\cap E=\{v\}$.
    Every other edge has its support contained in $T$, in $E$, or in $U$.
    Adjacent cuts, $v=u+1$, are included.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Membership in $T\cup E$ is equivalent to having coordinate less than
    $v+1$; membership in $U$ is equivalent to having coordinate at least
    $v+1$. Comparing the endpoints gives the four assertions for $D_u,D_v$.
    For any other edge, either $r<u$, $u<r<v$, or $v<r$.
    These cases give $r+2\le u+1$, the pair of inequalities
    $u+1\le r$ and $r+2\le v+1$, or $v+1\le r$, respectively.
    The corresponding interval containment places $D_r$ in $T$, $E$, or $U$.
    For adjacent cuts, $E=\{u+1\}=\{v\}$. The first two assertions give
    $D_u\cap E=\{u+1\}$ and the fourth gives $D_v\cap E=\{v\}$, so the
    crossing supports $D_u$ and $D_v$ share the single middle site.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Changed sites under interval displacement]
    \label{thm:chain_interval_symmdiff}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_interval_symmDiff_le_dist}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    For natural numbers $L,a,b,c,d$, let
    $[a,b)_L=\{t\in\{0,\ldots,L-1\}:a\le t<b\}$.
    Then
    \begin{align}
        |{\lbrack a,b)_L}\mathbin{\triangle}{\lbrack c,d)_L}| & \le |a-c|+|b-d|.
        \label{eq:chain_interval_symmdiff}
    \end{align}
    No ordering or containment assumptions on the endpoints are required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    A site belonging to exactly one interval lies between the two left
    endpoints or between the two right endpoints. Thus
    \begin{align}
        {\lbrack a,b)_L}\mathbin{\triangle}{\lbrack c,d)_L}
         & \subseteq [\min(a,c),\max(a,c))_L\notag \\
         & \qquad\cup [\min(b,d),\max(b,d))_L.
        \label{eq:chain_interval_endpoint_strips}
    \end{align}
    The two intervals in~(\ref{eq:chain_interval_endpoint_strips}) have at
    most $\max(a,c)-\min(a,c)=|a-c|$ and
    $\max(b,d)-\min(b,d)=|b-d|$ sites. Subadditivity of cardinality gives
    (\ref{eq:chain_interval_symmdiff}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Middle-region displacement within two windows]
    \label{thm:chain_two_window_middle_displacement}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_two_window_middle_symmDiff_le}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $L,a,b,\ell$ be natural numbers, let $0\le p,s<2\ell$, and let
    $\delta\in\{0,1\}$. Using the clipped intervals of
    Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_interval_symmdiff}, set
    \begin{align}
        E        & =[a+p+1,b+s+1)_L,\notag      \\
        E_\delta & =[a+\ell+\delta,b+\ell+1)_L.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_middle_regions}
    \end{align}
    Then $|E\mathbin{\triangle}E_\delta|\le2\ell$.
    When $a+\ell+1<b+\ell+1\le L$, write $i_1=a+\ell+1$ and
    $i_2=b+\ell+1$. In one-based site coordinates, $E_0$ is the block
    $\{i_1,\ldots,i_2\}$ in~\cite[Proposition~5.7]{Arad2016RigorousRG},
    whereas $E_1$ is the middle
    region between the cuts after $i_1$ and $i_2$ in its Theorem~4.2.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_interval_symmdiff}
    The inequalities $0\le p,s<2\ell$ and $\delta\in\{0,1\}$ give
    $|(p+1)-(\ell+\delta)|\le\ell$ and $|s-\ell|\le\ell$.
    Apply~(\ref{eq:chain_interval_symmdiff}) to the two intervals
    in~(\ref{eq:chain_two_window_middle_regions}) and add these bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Frequency vectors with two prescribed coordinates]
    \label{thm:chain_two_fixed_frequencies}
    \lean{Nat.card_fun_sum_eq_and_two_eq,
        Nat.card_fun_sum_eq_and_two_eq_of_lt,
        Nat.card_fun_sum_eq_and_two_eq_of_total}
    \leanok
    Let $I$ be a finite set, let $u,v\in I$ be distinct, and let
    $r,i,j$ be natural numbers. Define
    \begin{align}
        \mathcal F_{r,i,j}
         & =\left\{\alpha:I\to\mathbb N:
        \sum_{a\in I}\alpha(a)=r,\quad
        \alpha(u)=i,\quad\alpha(v)=j\right\}.\label{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_set}
    \end{align}
    If $r<i+j$, then the set in~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_set}) is empty.
    If $|I|=s+3$ for a natural number $s$, then
    \begin{align}
        |\mathcal F_{r,i,j}|
         & =\begin{cases}
                \displaystyle\binom{r-i-j+s}{s}, & i+j\le r, \\
                0,                               & r<i+j.
            \end{cases}\label{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_count}
    \end{align}
    Taking $r=m+i+j$ in~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_count}) gives
    $|\mathcal F_{m+i+j,i,j}|=\binom{m+s}{s}$
    for every natural number $m$.
    Thus the count is one when $s=0$, provided $i+j\le r$,
    and also when $r=i+j$.
    The grouped alphabet in \cite[Section~4.1]{Arad2016RigorousRG}
    has two windows of $2\ell$ bond labels and three bulk labels;
    for this alphabet the formula applies with $s=4\ell$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    For every $\alpha:I\to\mathbb N$,
    $\alpha(u)+\alpha(v)\le\sum_{a\in I}\alpha(a)$,
    proving the empty case.
    When the total is $m+i+j$, restricting to $I\setminus\{u,v\}$
    is a bijection between $\mathcal F_{m+i+j,i,j}$ and the
    nonnegative integer functions on $s+1$ labels whose sum is $m$.
    Its inverse reinserts $i$ at $u$ and $j$ at $v$.
    Identifying these functions with multisets of cardinality $m$
    gives $\binom{m+s}{s}$.
    When $i+j\le r$, substituting $m=r-i-j$ gives~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_count}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform bound for frequency slices]
    \label{thm:chain_fixed_frequency_uniform_bound}
    \lean{Nat.card_fun_sum_eq_and_two_eq_le}
    \leanok
    Let $I$ have cardinality $s+3$, and let $u,v\in I$ be distinct.
    For natural numbers $r,i,j$, let $\mathcal F_{r,i,j}$ be the set
    in~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_set}). Then
    \begin{align}
        |\mathcal F_{r,i,j}| & \le (r+1)^s.
        \label{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_uniform_bound}
    \end{align}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_two_fixed_frequencies}
    If $r<i+j$, the set is empty. Otherwise put $m=r-i-j$.
    The exact count in~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_count}) and the
    elementary binomial estimate give
    \begin{align}
        |\mathcal F_{r,i,j}|=\binom{m+s}{s}
         & \le (m+1)^s\le(r+1)^s.
        \label{eq:chain_frequency_binomial_bound}
    \end{align}
    Combining~(\ref{eq:chain_frequency_binomial_bound}) with the empty case
    proves~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_uniform_bound}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Frequency slices through a prescribed degree]
    \label{thm:chain_fixed_frequency_degree_sum}
    \lean{Nat.sum_card_fun_sum_eq_and_two_eq_le}
    \leanok
    Let $I$ be a finite set of cardinality $s+3$, with $s\in\mathbb N$,
    and let $u,v\in I$ be distinct. For natural numbers $k,i,j$, use
    $\mathcal F_{r,i,j}$ from~(\ref{eq:chain_fixed_frequency_set}). Then
    \begin{align}
        \sum_{r=0}^{k}|\mathcal F_{r,i,j}|
         & \le (k+1)^{s+1}.
        \label{eq:chain_frequency_degree_sum}
    \end{align}
    All degrees from $0$ through $k$ are included. For an alphabet of
    $4\ell+3$ labels, the exponent is $4\ell+1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_fixed_frequency_uniform_bound}
    For each $0\le r\le k$,
    Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_fixed_frequency_uniform_bound} gives
    $|\mathcal F_{r,i,j}|\le(r+1)^s\le(k+1)^s$.
    Summing over the $k+1$ degrees gives
    $(k+1)(k+1)^s=(k+1)^{s+1}$.
\end{proof}

The following scalar estimates are auxiliaries for the two-window argument
of \cite[Proposition~5.7]{Arad2016RigorousRG}. For natural
$k,d,\ell,b$, write
\begin{align}
    C_{k,d,\ell,b}
     & =(2\ell)^2(b+1)^2(k+1)^{4\ell+1}\notag            \\
     & \qquad\cdot\bigl(2(k+1)\bigr)^{4b+2}d^{4b+4\ell}.
    \label{eq:chain_two_window_count_factor}
\end{align}
The full alphabet of $4\ell+3$ labels is retained, consisting of the two
windows and the three bulk labels.

\begin{theorem}[Summing the two-window counting factors]
    \label{thm:chain_two_window_count_sum}
    \lean{Nat.two_window_rank_sum_le_factor}
    \leanok
    Let $k,d,\ell,b\in\mathbb N$ with $d\ge1$ and $b\le k$. Then
    \begin{align}
         & (2\ell)^2
        \sum_{\substack{0\le r\le k  \\0\le i,j\le b}}
        (r+1)^{4\ell}
        \binom{r+i+1}{2i+1}\binom{r+j+1}{2j+1}
        d^{2(i+j)+4\ell}\notag       \\
         & \qquad\le C_{k,d,\ell,b}.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_count_sum}
    \end{align}
    The sum ranges over every natural triple in the indicated ranges,
    including degree zero. The binomial factors retain the upper arguments
    $r+i+1$ and $r+j+1$ printed in
    \cite[Proposition~5.7]{Arad2016RigorousRG}; the count of commuting gaps
    is recorded in~\cite{gap:aklv13_interpolation_and_word_count}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    For $r\le k$ and $i\le b\le k$, the elementary binomial estimate gives
    \begin{align}
        \binom{r+i+1}{2i+1}
         & \le (r+i+1)^{2i+1}
        \le\bigl(2(k+1)\bigr)^{2b+1}.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_gap_bound}
    \end{align}
    Apply~(\ref{eq:chain_two_window_gap_bound}) to both binomials.
    Also $(r+1)^{4\ell}\le(k+1)^{4\ell}$ and
    $d^{2(i+j)+4\ell}\le d^{4b+4\ell}$, since $d\ge1$.
    Each summand is therefore at most
    $(k+1)^{4\ell}\bigl(2(k+1)\bigr)^{4b+2}d^{4b+4\ell}$.
    There are $(k+1)(b+1)^2$ triples. Multiplying by this number and by
    $(2\ell)^2$ gives the product
    (\ref{eq:chain_two_window_count_factor}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[An explicit bound for the counting product]
    \label{thm:chain_two_window_count_power}
    \lean{Nat.two_window_rank_factor_le_pow}
    \leanok
    Let $k,d,\ell,b\in\mathbb N$ with $k\ge1$, $d\ge2$,
    $\ell\ge1$ and $b\le k$. For the product in
    (\ref{eq:chain_two_window_count_factor}),
    \begin{align}
        C_{k,d,\ell,b}
         & \le(dk)^{17(\ell+b)}.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_count_power}
    \end{align}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    Put $X=dk\ge2$. The hypotheses give $d\le X$,
    $b+1\le k+1\le X$, $2\ell\le2^\ell\le X^\ell$ and
    $2(k+1)\le X^2$. Substituting these bounds into
    (\ref{eq:chain_two_window_count_factor}) yields
    \begin{align}
        C_{k,d,\ell,b}
         & \le X^{10\ell+12b+7}
        \le X^{17(\ell+b)}.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_count_exponent}
    \end{align}
    The last step uses $\ell\ge1$, which implies
    $10\ell+12b+7\le17(\ell+b)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Distance and spheres on a finite path]
    \label{lem:chain_path_metric_spheres}
    \lean{SimpleGraph.pathGraph_edist, SimpleGraph.pathGraph_card_sphere_le_two}
    \leanok
    Let $L$ be a natural number, and let $P_L$ be the graph on
    $\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$ joining consecutive coordinates. For vertices $x,y$ and
    every natural number $d$,
    \begin{align}
        d_{P_L}(x,y)                     & =|x-y|, \\
        \bigl|\{y:d_{P_L}(x,y)=d\}\bigr| & \le2.
    \end{align}
    Thus the sphere growth bound holds with coefficient $2$ and exponent $0$,
    independently of $L$. This concerns the contiguous chain setting of
    Section~1 of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    Each edge changes the coordinate by one, so the displacement of a walk is
    at most its length. Taking the infimum over walks gives one inequality.
    In the other direction, successive coordinates between $x$ and $y$ stay
    inside the chain and give a walk of length $|x-y|$. A site at distance $d$
    from $x$ has coordinate $x-d$ or $x+d$; discarding coordinates outside the
    chain leaves at most two sites. At $d=0$ the candidates coincide. When
    $L=0$ there are no vertices, so the assertions are vacuous.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Regional forms of a nearest-neighbour bond array]
    \label{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_factors}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.exists_threeSplit_edge_factors}
    \leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_supports,lem:peps_frame_split_forms}
    Let $L,q,u,v$ be natural numbers with $u<v$ and $v+1<L$.
    For each bond index $0\le r<L-1$, let $H_r$ be a complex operator
    supported on $D_r=\{r,r+1\}$. Use the partition $T,E,U$ of
    Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_supports}, and write $\widetilde H_r$
    for $H_r$ in the three-region coordinates of
    Lemma~\ref{lem:peps_frame_split_forms}.
    There are arrays $H^{\mathrm L}_r,H^{\mathrm M}_r,H^{\mathrm R}_r$
    of operators on $\mathcal H_T,\mathcal H_E,\mathcal H_U$, respectively,
    and crossing factors $A_u$ on $\mathcal H_T\otimes\mathcal H_E$ and
    $B_v$ on $\mathcal H_E\otimes\mathcal H_U$ with the following properties.
    For every $r\ne u,v$,
    \begin{align}
        \widetilde H_r
         & =(H^{\mathrm L}_r\otimes\id_E)\otimes\id_U
        +(\id_T\otimes H^{\mathrm M}_r)\otimes\id_U\notag \\
         & \quad+\id_{TE}\otimes H^{\mathrm R}_r.
        \label{eq:chain_two_cut_ordinary_forms}
    \end{align}
    The two distinguished operators satisfy
    \begin{align}
        \widetilde H_u & =A_u\otimes\id_U,
                       & \operatorname{OSR}(A_u) & \le q^2,\notag \\
        \widetilde H_v & =\id_T\otimes B_v,
                       & \operatorname{OSR}(B_v) & \le q^2.
        \label{eq:chain_two_cut_crossing_forms}
    \end{align}
    All tensors are read in the same left-associated coordinates, using
    canonical reassociation for $\id_T\otimes B_v$.
    The arrays and crossing factors are chosen simultaneously for the given
    bond array and cuts. The local dimension may be zero, and the cuts may
    be adjacent. These are tensor forms for the local terms in
    \cite[Sections~4.1 and~5.2]{Arad2016RigorousRG}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_supports,lem:peps_frame_split_forms,
        thm:peps_supported_left_factor_rank,thm:peps_supported_right_factor_rank}
    The support classification places every ordinary bond in $T$, $E$ or
    $U$. Apply the corresponding single-region part of
    Lemma~\ref{lem:peps_frame_split_forms}, putting zero in the other two
    array entries. Making these choices over the finite bond index set gives
    the arrays in~(\ref{eq:chain_two_cut_ordinary_forms}).

    For the first crossing, the classification gives
    $D_u\subseteq T\cup E$ and $D_u\cap T=\{u\}$.
    Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_supported_left_factor_rank} therefore supplies
    the same $A_u$ for its tensor identity and the bound
    $q^{2|D_u\cap T|}=q^2$.
    For the second crossing, $D_v\cap T=\varnothing$ and
    $D_v\cap E=\{v\}$, so
    Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_supported_right_factor_rank} supplies the same
    $B_v$ for its associated tensor identity and its bound
    $q^{2|D_v\cap E|}=q^2$.
    These are~(\ref{eq:chain_two_cut_crossing_forms}), including $q=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Regional forms of two windows and three bulk operators]
    \label{thm:chain_two_window_factors}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.exists_threeSplit_two_window_factors}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,lem:peps_frame_split_forms}
    Let $L,q,\ell,a,b$ be natural numbers with $a+2\ell\le b$ and
    $b+2\ell\le L-1$. Consider the $4\ell+3$ distinct labels
    \begin{align}
        \mathcal I
         & =\{x_r:0\le r<2\ell\}\sqcup\{y_r:0\le r<2\ell\}
        \sqcup\{\lambda,\mu,\rho\}.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_alphabet}
    \end{align}
    For each $\alpha\in\mathcal I$, let $F_\alpha$ be a complex operator
    on the chain. Suppose that $F_{x_r}$ is supported on $[a+r,a+r+2)$
    and $F_{y_r}$ on $[b+r,b+r+2)$, and that the three independently
    given bulk operators satisfy
    \begin{align}
        F_\lambda & \text{ is supported on }[0,a+1),\notag       \\
        F_\mu     & \text{ is supported on }[a+2\ell,b+1),\notag \\
        F_\rho    & \text{ is supported on }[b+2\ell,L).
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_bulk_supports}
    \end{align}
    The labels remain distinct when operators coincide or vanish.
    Choose integers $0\le p,s<2\ell$, put $u=a+p$ and $v=b+s$, and set
    \begin{align}
        T & =[0,u+1),\notag   \\
        E & =[u+1,v+1),\notag \\
        U & =[v+1,L).
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_regions}
    \end{align}
    Write $\widetilde F_\alpha$ for $F_\alpha$ in the three-region
    coordinates of Lemma~\ref{lem:peps_frame_split_forms}.
    There are families $F^{\mathrm L}_\alpha,F^{\mathrm M}_\alpha,
        F^{\mathrm R}_\alpha$ of operators on $\mathcal H_T,\mathcal H_E,
        \mathcal H_U$, respectively, and operators $A$ on
    $\mathcal H_T\otimes\mathcal H_E$ and $B$ on
    $\mathcal H_E\otimes\mathcal H_U$, chosen simultaneously, such that
    for every $\alpha\ne x_p,y_s$,
    \begin{align}
        \widetilde F_\alpha
         & =(F^{\mathrm L}_\alpha\otimes\id_E)\otimes\id_U
        +(\id_T\otimes F^{\mathrm M}_\alpha)\otimes\id_U\notag \\
         & \quad+\id_{TE}\otimes F^{\mathrm R}_\alpha.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_ordinary_forms}
    \end{align}
    The same $A$ and $B$ satisfy
    \begin{align}
        \widetilde F_{x_p}    & =A\otimes\id_U,\notag  \\
        \operatorname{OSR}(A) & \le q^2,\notag         \\
        \widetilde F_{y_s}    & =\id_T\otimes B,\notag \\
        \operatorname{OSR}(B) & \le q^2.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_crossing_forms}
    \end{align}
    All tensors use the same left-associated coordinates, with canonical
    reassociation for $\id_T\otimes B$. The local dimension may be zero.
    When $a+2\ell=b$, the middle bulk support is the singleton $\{b\}$.
    This is a tensor decomposition for the two-window grouping in
    \cite[Sections~4.1 and~5.2]{Arad2016RigorousRG}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_supports,lem:peps_frame_split_forms,
        thm:peps_supported_left_factor_rank,thm:peps_supported_right_factor_rank}
    The hypotheses give $u<v$ and $v+1<L$.
    Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_two_cut_edge_supports} places each window edge
    other than $u,v$ wholly in one of $T,E,U$.
    The three bulk supports satisfy
    \begin{align}
        {[0,a+1)}       & \subseteq T,\notag \\
        {[a+2\ell,b+1)} & \subseteq E,\notag \\
        {[b+2\ell,L)}   & \subseteq U.
        \label{eq:chain_two_window_bulk_containments}
    \end{align}
    Thus every operator other than $F_{x_p},F_{y_s}$ is supported in one
    region. Apply the corresponding single-region form of
    Lemma~\ref{lem:peps_frame_split_forms}, putting zero in the other two
    regional factors. Choosing these factors over $\mathcal I$ gives the
    three families in~(\ref{eq:chain_two_window_ordinary_forms}).

    The first crossing support is contained in $T\cup E$ and meets $T$
    in the single site $u$. Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_supported_left_factor_rank}
    supplies $A$ with both its tensor identity and the bound $q^2$.
    The second crossing support is disjoint from $T$ and meets $E$ in
    the single site $v$. Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_supported_right_factor_rank}
    supplies $B$ with both its tensor identity and the bound $q^2$.
    These are~(\ref{eq:chain_two_window_crossing_forms}), including $q=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Marginal moment and tail bounds at a cut]
    \label{lem:chain_marginal_tail}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.surprisalTail_regionState_le,
        Entropy.log_surprisalMoment_cut_le}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $\Omega$ be a gapped ground vector, with gap $\Delta>0$, of a
    finite-range Hamiltonian with term norms at most $J\ge0$ on the chain, and
    let $A$ be any set of sites. Let $K$ be the surprisal $-\log p$ of the
    eigenvalue $p$ of the reduced state on $A$ drawn with probability $p$, so
    that $\mathbb E K=S(A)$. For every real $u$ with
    $|u|\le1/(32\sqrt{(1+J/\Delta)\,\mathcal B_A})$,
    \begin{align}
        \log\mathbb E e^{uK}
        \le uS(A)+512e\frac J\Delta\mathcal B_Au^2.\notag
    \end{align}
    For every real $w$,
    \begin{align}
        \Pr\{|K-S(A)|>w\}\le
        \min\Bigl\{1,\ 2e^{e/2}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{w}{32\sqrt{(1+J/\Delta)\,
                \mathcal B_A}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.
    \end{align}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    These are the log-moment and tail bounds of Lemma~3.1 of
    \cite{OpenAI2026AreaLaw}, valid on any finite tensor product: each term
    acts as the identity off its support, is
    Hermitian and has norm at most $J$, and the ground vector is a unit
    eigenvector with the projector gap. The chain model supplies exactly these
    hypotheses.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Doubled chain Hamiltonian]
    \label{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.doubled}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,def:qc_site_pair_coordinates}
    Let $L,q,R$ be natural numbers, let $J$ be real, and let
    $H=\sum_X h_X$ be a range-$R$ chain Hamiltonian with local dimension $q$
    and $\|h_X\|\le J$. Pair the two copies at each original site by the
    bijection $p$ of Definition~\ref{def:qc_site_pair_coordinates}, with
    $p(\sigma,\tau)(i)=q\sigma(i)+\tau(i)$. Write $p_*M$ for simultaneous
    relabelling of the rows and columns of a matrix $M$ by $p$. The
    \emph{doubled Hamiltonian} has local dimension $q^2$ and interactions
    \begin{align}
        \widehat h_X=p_*(h_X\otimes\mathbb 1+\mathbb 1\otimes h_X).
    \end{align}
    Each $\widehat h_X$ is Hermitian, is supported on the same set $X$, and
    satisfies $\|\widehat h_X\|\le2J$. Thus the range remains $R$.
    This is the two-copy construction in the paragraph ``Two-sided Gaussian
    filtering'' of \cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:qc_local_circuit,thm:area_law_supported_identity,thm:qc_site_pair_tensor_support}
    By the matrix-entry characterization, $h_X$ belongs to the algebra
    supported on $X$; the identity belongs to that algebra as well. The tensor-product support identity,
    applied to each summand, gives support on $X\cup X=X$, and the
    matrix-entry characterization gives the required support condition.
    Taking adjoints preserves each summand and commutes with relabelling.
    Relabelling preserves the operator norm, and tensoring with an identity
    does not increase it. Hence
    \begin{align}
        \|\widehat h_X\|
        \le\|h_X\otimes\mathbb 1\|+\|\mathbb 1\otimes h_X\|
        \le\|h_X\|+\|h_X\|\le2J.
    \end{align}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Operator of the doubled Hamiltonian]
    \label{lem:chain_doubled_operator}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.doubled_operator}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian}
    Under the hypotheses of Definition~\ref{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian},
    the sum of the doubled interactions satisfies
    \begin{align}
        \widehat H=\sum_X\widehat h_X
        =p_*(H\otimes\mathbb 1+\mathbb 1\otimes H).
    \end{align}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian}
    Sum the defining interaction identity over the original supports $X$.
    Relabelling is linear, and the tensor product is linear in each factor.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Regionally swapped doubled Hamiltonian]
    \label{def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.swappedDoubled,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.swappedDoubled_operator}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian,def:peps_frame_sheet_swap}
    Let $H=\sum_Xh_X$ be a range-$R$ Hamiltonian on the contiguous chain
    $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$, with local dimension $q$ and
    $\|h_X\|\le J$, where $L,q,R$ are natural numbers and $J$ is real.
    For any $D\subseteq I$, let $F=p_*F_D$ in the paired coordinates of
    Definition~\ref{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian}. The regionally swapped
    doubled Hamiltonian has interactions and sum
    \begin{align}
        \widehat h'_X & =F\widehat h_XF^\dagger,\notag              \\
        \widehat H'   & =\sum_X\widehat h'_X=F\widehat H F^\dagger.
        \label{eq:chain_swapped_doubled_operator}
    \end{align}
    Each $\widehat h'_X$ is Hermitian, is supported on the same set $X$,
    and has norm at most $2J$. Thus this is a range-$R$ chain Hamiltonian
    with local dimension $q^2$ and interaction bound $2J$, also when
    $q=0$ or $L=0$.
\end{definition}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian,thm:area_law_supported_identity,
        thm:peps_sheet_swap_conjugate_support,thm:peps_frame_sheet_swap_norm}
    The support identity for the conjugated doubled interaction gives
    support on $X$, and taking adjoints preserves Hermiticity.
    Since $\|F\|\le1$, the doubled interaction estimate gives
    \begin{align}
        \|F\widehat h_XF^\dagger\|
        \le\|\widehat h_X\|\le2J.
    \end{align}
    Distributing left and right multiplication over the finite sum proves
    (\ref{eq:chain_swapped_doubled_operator}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The ground vectors of the doubled and swapped chains]
    \label{thm:chain_doubled_swapped_ground_gap}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.doubled_swappedDoubled_isGappedGroundState}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian}
    In the setting of Definition~\ref{def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian},
    let $E_0,\Delta$ be real with $\Delta>0$, and suppose the original
    vector $\Omega$ satisfies
    \begin{align}
        \|\Omega\|     & =1,\notag                                          \\
        H\Omega        & =E_0\Omega,\notag                                  \\
        H-E_0\mathbb 1 & \ge\Delta(\mathbb 1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|).
    \end{align}
    Define the vectors in the paired physical coordinates by
    $\Psi(p(\sigma,\tau))=\Omega(\sigma)\Omega(\tau)$ and
    $\Phi=F\Psi$. Then
    \begin{align}
        \|\Psi\|        & =\|\Phi\|=1,\notag                                   \\
        \widehat H\Psi  & =2E_0\Psi,\notag                                     \\
        \widehat H'\Phi & =2E_0\Phi,\notag                                     \\
        \widehat H-2E_0\mathbb 1
                        & \ge\Delta(\mathbb 1-|\Psi\rangle\langle\Psi|),\notag \\
        \widehat H'-2E_0\mathbb 1
                        & \ge\Delta(\mathbb 1-|\Phi\rangle\langle\Phi|).
    \end{align}
    In particular, these vectors give the ground lines of the two
    Hamiltonians, with common ground energy $2E_0$ and gap at least
    $\Delta$. This is the contiguous-chain assertion in the paragraph
    ``Two-sided Gaussian filtering'' of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional},
    before the filtering construction; the distinction from arbitrary
    finite induced domains is recorded
    in~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{lem:chain_doubled_operator,def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian,
        thm:permutation_matrix_unitary,thm:mpu_reindex_unitary}
    Put $P=|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|$, $Q=\mathbb 1-P$ and
    $G=H-E_0\mathbb 1-\Delta Q\ge0$. Normalization gives $Q\ge0$.
    Before pairing coordinates, the doubled defect is
    \begin{align}
        H\otimes\mathbb 1+\mathbb 1\otimes H-2E_0\mathbb 1
        -\Delta(\mathbb 1-P\otimes P)
         & =G\otimes\mathbb 1+\mathbb 1\otimes G+\Delta Q\otimes Q
        \ge0.
    \end{align}
    The tensor-square vector satisfies
    \begin{align}
        (H\otimes\mathbb 1+\mathbb 1\otimes H)(\Omega\otimes\Omega)
         & =2E_0\,\Omega\otimes\Omega,
         &
        \|\Omega\otimes\Omega\|
         & =\|\Omega\|^2=1.
    \end{align}
    Pairing coordinates is unitary, so the same norm, eigen-equation and
    positive defect hold for $\Psi$ and $\widehat H$.
    The regional swap is a permutation matrix; after pairing it remains
    unitary, $F^\dagger F=\mathbb 1$. Since $\widehat H'=F\widehat HF^\dagger$,
    \begin{align}
        \widehat H'\Phi
         & =F\widehat HF^\dagger F\Psi=F\widehat H\Psi=2E_0\Phi,
         &
        \|\Phi\|
         & =\|F\Psi\|=\|\Psi\|=1,
    \end{align}
    and
    \begin{align}
         & \widehat H'-2E_0\mathbb 1
        -\Delta(\mathbb 1-|\Phi\rangle\langle\Phi|)\notag \\
         & \qquad=F\bigl(\widehat H-2E_0\mathbb 1
        -\Delta(\mathbb 1-|\Psi\rangle\langle\Psi|)\bigr)F^\dagger
        \ge0.
    \end{align}
    Finally, a positive projector gap forces every vector at the ground
    energy to lie in the span of the displayed unit vector, giving the
    asserted ground lines.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Supports contained in a prescribed set]
    \label{thm:chain_supports_in_window}
    \lean{
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_filter_subset_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_filter_subset_le_pow_of_card_le
    }
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $W$ be a set of chain sites with $|W|\le n$. At most $2^n$
    admissible interaction supports are contained in $W$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    These supports form a subfamily of the power set of $W$, whose
    cardinality is $2^{|W|}\le2^n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Interactions split by an interval]
    \label{lem:chain_crossing_count}
    \lean{
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.IsChainSupport.subset_window_of_splits,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.chainCrossConst,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_crossingTerms_interval_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.cutBudgetConst,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.one_le_cutBudgetConst,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.chainBudgetConst,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.one_le_chainBudgetConst,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.supportDim_le_real,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.log_sq_supportDim_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.cutLogBudget_interval_le
    }
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,thm:chain_supports_in_window,thm:chain_single_splitting}
    Let $X=[a,b)$ be an interval of the chain. A support of range $R$ that
    meets both $X$ and its complement lies in the window $[a-R,a+R)$ or in the
    window $[b-R,b+R)$, each of at most $2R$ sites. Hence at most
    \begin{align}
        N_R=2\cdot2^{2R}
    \end{align}
    supports are split by $X$, and the cut budget of $X$ is at most
    \begin{align}
        B_R=1+N_R\log^2\bigl(e\,q^{R+1}\bigr).
    \end{align}
    Both bounds are independent of the interval, and no support of range $R$
    is split by two of the shells $[l-(j+1)R,\,r+(j+1)R)$, $j\ge0$, of an
    interval $[l,r)$. For a natural number $N$ put
    $B(N)=1+N\log^2\bigl(e\,q^{R+1}\bigr)$, so that $B(N)\ge1$ and
    $B_R=B(N_R)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    A split support contains a site $v$ in $X$ and a site $w$ outside $X$
    with $|v-w|\le R$. If $w<a$ then every site $x$ of the support satisfies
    $a-R\le v-R\le x\le w+R<a+R$; if $w\ge b$ then $b-R\le w-R\le x\le v+R<b+R$.
    A window of at most $2R$ sites has at most $2^{2R}$ subsets. The budget
    bound follows from the crossing count and the dimension bound $q^{R+1}$. If
    a support met the shell with index $j$ and left the shell with index
    $j'>j$, two of its sites would be at distance at least $R+1$.
\end{proof}

% Source: part1_arealaw.tex:706--709, with eq:theta and eq:mgf:152--166.
\begin{theorem}[Consecutive-window interactions split by an interval]
    \label{thm:chain_consecutive_window_count}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.card_crossingTerms_interval_le_of_consecutive_terms}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $L,q,R$ be natural numbers, let $J$ be real, and let
    $H=\sum_X h_X$ be a finite-range Hamiltonian on the chain of $L$ sites.
    Suppose that for every $X$ with $h_X\ne0$ there is a natural number $a$
    such that $a+R+1\le L$ and $X=[a,a+R+1)$.
    For natural numbers $l,r$, put $A=[l,r)\cap\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$. Then
    \begin{align}
        \bigl|\{X:h_X\ne0,\ X\cap A\ne\varnothing,
        \ X\setminus A\ne\varnothing\}\bigr|\le2R.\notag
    \end{align}
    This is the consecutive-window counting refinement in the remark on
    sharper constants of~\cite[Part~I]{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    A full window is determined by its least site $a$. If it crosses the cut
    at position $c$, then $a<c\le a+R$, so its start belongs to $[c-R,c)$,
    which has at most $R$ sites. A window split by $A$ crosses at least one
    of the cuts at $l$ and $r$. Its start therefore lies in
    $[l-R,l)\cup{[r-R,r)}$, a set of at most $2R$ sites. All intervals are
    clipped to the chain, and natural subtraction is truncated at zero.
    When $R=0$, or when $A$ is empty, no window is split.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The cut budget for consecutive-window interactions]
    \label{thm:chain_window_cut_budget}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.cutLogBudget_interval_le_of_consecutive_terms}
    \leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_consecutive_window_count}
    Let $q\ge1$, and suppose that every nonzero interaction is assigned to
    a full window of $R+1$ consecutive sites contained in the chain.
    For every coordinate interval $A$, put
    \begin{align}
        \mathcal B(A)
         & =1+\sum_{\substack{X:\ h_X\ne0,\ X\cap A\ne\varnothing \\
                X\setminus A\ne\varnothing}}
        \log^2(e q^{|X|}).\notag
    \end{align}
    Then $\mathcal B(A)\le1+2R\log^2(e q^{R+1})$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_consecutive_window_count,lem:chain_crossing_count}
    At most $2R$ nonzero interactions are split by $A$. Each support has
    at most $R+1$ sites. Since $q\ge1$, each summand is at most
    $\log^2(e q^{R+1})$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Marginal moments for consecutive-window interactions]
    \label{thm:chain_consecutive_window_moment}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.log_surprisalMoment_interval_le_of_consecutive_terms}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $L,R$ be natural numbers, let $q\ge1$ be an integer, and let
    $J\ge0$ and $\Delta>0$ be real. Let $H=\sum_X h_X$ be a
    finite-range Hamiltonian on the chain of $L$ sites. Suppose that for
    every $X$ with $h_X\ne0$ there is a natural number $a$ with
    $a+R+1\le L$ and $X=[a,a+R+1)$.
    Let $E_0$ be real and let $\Omega$ be a unit vector satisfying
    $H\Omega=E_0\Omega$ and
    $H-E_0\ge\Delta(1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|)$.
    For natural numbers $l,r$, let $\rho_A$ be the reduced state on
    $A=[l,r)\cap\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$, and let $K$ take the value $-\log p$
    with probability $p$ over the positive eigenvalues of $\rho_A$.
    Put $B=1+2R\log^2(e\,q^{R+1})$. For every real $u$ with
    $|u|\le1/(32\sqrt{(1+J/\Delta)B})$,
    \begin{align}
        \log\mathbb E e^{uK}
        \le uS(A)+512e\frac J\Delta B u^2.\notag
    \end{align}
    This is the marginal moment estimate with the consecutive-window count
    from the remark on sharper constants of
    \cite[Part~I]{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,thm:chain_consecutive_window_count,lem:chain_crossing_count,lem:chain_marginal_tail}
    Restrict the interaction sum to $h_X\ne0$. The sum remains exactly $H$,
    and its terms retain their supports, Hermiticity and norm bound $J$.
    The same unit eigenvector and projector gap therefore apply.
    The crossing count and $\dim\mathcal H_X\le q^{R+1}$ bound the budget
    for this restricted family by $B$. Apply the marginal moment estimate
    to that family. Since $J/\Delta\ge0$, replacing its budget by $B$
    decreases the allowed radius and increases the quadratic error.
\end{proof}

% Source: part1_arealaw.tex:388--403, single-splitting lemma.
\begin{theorem}[Single splitting of nested chain intervals]
    \label{thm:chain_single_splitting}
    \lean{
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.single_splitting_shells,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.not_splits_two_shells
    }
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $L,R,l,r$ be natural numbers. For each natural number $i$, define
    the coordinate interval
    \begin{align}
        X_i=[l-iR,r+iR)\cap\{0,\ldots,L-1\}.\notag
    \end{align}
    Let $Y$ be a set of chain sites with $|x-y|\le R$ for all $x,y\in Y$.
    Suppose $Y$ meets both $X_j$ and its complement for some $j\ge0$.
    For every natural number $i$,
    \begin{align}
        i>j & \ \Longrightarrow\ Y\subseteq X_i,\notag        \\
        i<j & \ \Longrightarrow\ Y\cap X_i=\varnothing,\notag \\
        Y\cap X_i\ne\varnothing\ \text{and}\ Y\setminus X_i\ne\varnothing
            & \ \Longrightarrow\ i=j.\notag
    \end{align}
    In particular, $Y$ is split by at most one of these intervals, including
    $X_0$. For a nonempty initial interval, these coordinate intervals are
    its metric enlargements by $iR$, so this gives the single-splitting
    lemma of \cite[Part~I]{TNLean2026OneDimensional}. The coordinate
    statement also holds for arbitrary endpoints $l,r$ and for $R=0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    Choose $v\in Y\cap X_j$ and $w\in Y\setminus X_j$.
    If $i>j$, then $iR\ge jR+R$. For every $y\in Y$,
    \begin{align}
        l-iR & \le l-jR-R\le v-R\le y,\notag \\
        y    & \le v+R<r+jR+R\le r+iR.\notag
    \end{align}
    Thus $Y\subseteq X_i$. If $i<j$ and $z\in Y\cap X_i$, the same
    comparison with $z$ in place of $v$ and the indices $i,j$ exchanged
    would give $Y\subseteq X_j$, contradicting $w\notin X_j$.
    Hence $Y\cap X_i=\varnothing$. Neither a containing interval nor a
    disjoint interval splits $Y$, which proves uniqueness. Taking the
    positive indices $j+1$ gives the corresponding assertion for the
    family starting with radius $R$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Logarithmic dependence of the interval cut budget]
    \label{thm:chain_budget_log_sq}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.chainBudgetConst_le_mul_log_sq}
    \leanok
    \uses{lem:chain_crossing_count}
    Let $R$ be a nonnegative integer and put $N_R=2\cdot2^{2R}$ and
    $B_R(q)=1+N_R\log^2(e q^{R+1})$. For every integer $q\ge2$,
    \begin{align}
        B_R(q)\le\frac{B_R(2)}{(\log2)^2}(\log q)^2.
    \end{align}
    Here $N_R$ is the interval crossing bound for the
    family of all admissible supports, as in the cut-budget definition of
    \cite[Part~I]{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
    The remark on sharper constants in that part describes its application
    to the logarithmic dependence of the area-law constant.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{lem:chain_crossing_count}
    Put $c=\log2>0$ and $t=\log q\ge c$. The identity
    $\log(e q^{R+1})=1+(R+1)t$ gives
    $c(1+(R+1)t)\le t(1+(R+1)c)$.
    Both sides are nonnegative. Squaring, multiplying by $N_R$ and adding
    $c^2\le t^2$ yields
    \begin{align}
        c^2 B_R(q)
         & =c^2+N_R\bigl(c(1+(R+1)t)\bigr)^2 \notag \\
         & \le t^2+N_R\bigl(t(1+(R+1)c)\bigr)^2
        =t^2 B_R(2). \notag
    \end{align}
    Divide by $c^2>0$.
\end{proof}


% Source: part1_arealaw.tex:280--287, rem:renyi.
\begin{theorem}[Rényi entropies near order one]
    \label{thm:chain_renyi_near_one}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.near_one_renyi_entropy_interval_bounds}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,lem:chain_crossing_count,lem:chain_marginal_tail}
    Let $\Omega$ be a gapped ground vector, with gap $\Delta>0$, of a
    finite-range chain Hamiltonian with local dimension $q\ge1$ and term
    norms at most $J\ge0$. For an interval $X$, let $\rho_X$ be its reduced
    density matrix and put
    \begin{align}
        \vartheta=J/\Delta,\qquad
        B_R=1+N_R\log^2(e q^{R+1}),\qquad
        u_0=\frac1{32\sqrt{(1+\vartheta)B_R}}.\notag
    \end{align}
    For $0<\alpha<1$, define
    \begin{align}
        S_\alpha(X)=\frac{\log\operatorname{Tr}(\rho_X^\alpha)}{1-\alpha},
        \qquad S_1(X)=S(X).\notag
    \end{align}
    Zero eigenvalues contribute zero to the trace. For every
    $\alpha\in[1-u_0,1]$,
    \begin{align}
        S(X)               & \le S_\alpha(X)
        \le S(X)+512e\vartheta B_R(1-\alpha),\notag               \\
        |S_\alpha(X)-S(X)| & \le16e\sqrt{(1+\vartheta)B_R}.\notag
    \end{align}
    This is the near-one Rényi bound in Part~I of
    \cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    Write $p_i$ for the eigenvalues of $\rho_X$ and set
    $M(u)=\sum_i p_i\exp(-u\log p_i)$, with zero terms interpreted as zero.
    Since $p_i\ge0$ and $\sum_i p_i=1$, Jensen's inequality gives
    \begin{align}
        \exp(uS(X))\le M(u).\notag
    \end{align}
    The physical log-moment estimate, with the interval cut budget bounded
    by $B_R$, gives
    \begin{align}
        uS(X)\le\log M(u)
        \le uS(X)+512e\vartheta B_Ru^2
        \qquad(0\le u\le u_0).\notag
    \end{align}
    Put $s=\sqrt{(1+\vartheta)B_R}\ge1$. Then $u_0\le1/32$, so the
    orders in the statement are positive. For $u=1-\alpha>0$,
    $M(u)=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho_X^\alpha)$, including when $\rho_X$
    has a kernel. Dividing by $u$ proves the first pair of inequalities.
    Moreover,
    \begin{align}
        512e\vartheta B_Ru
        \le16e\frac{\vartheta B_R}{s}
        \le16es,\notag
    \end{align}
    which proves the absolute bound. At $u=0$, all conclusions follow
    from $S_1(X)=S(X)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Supports split by an arbitrary region of a chain]
    \label{lem:chain_boundary_crossing_count}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.IsChainSupport.exists_boundary_window_of_split,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_crossingTerms_le_mul_card_boundaryEdges}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,lem:chain_entropy_inequalities}
    Let $L,R$ be natural numbers, let $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$ be the
    contiguous chain, and let $D\subseteq I$. Write $\partial D$ for the
    positions $p$ with $p+1<L$ such that exactly one of $p,p+1$ belongs
    to $D$. For $p\in\partial D$ put
    \begin{align}
        W_p=I\cap[\max\{0,p+1-R\},\,p+1+R).
    \end{align}
    If a nonempty support $X\subseteq I$ of diameter at most $R$ meets
    both $D$ and $I\setminus D$, then $X\subseteq W_p$ for some boundary
    edge $p\in\partial D$. Consequently,
    \begin{align}
        \#\{X\subseteq I:\ X\ne\varnothing,\ \operatorname{diam}X\le R,
        \ X\cap D\ne\varnothing,\ X\setminus D\ne\varnothing\}
        \le 2^{2R}|\partial D|.
    \end{align}
    No interval assumption on $D$ is needed. The conclusions include the
    empty chain and $R=0$; in either case there is no split support.
    This is the boundary-window count underlying the interval estimate
    in Section~2, equation~\textup{(count)}, and the boundary count in the
    paragraph ``Two-sided Gaussian filtering'' of
    \cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,lem:chain_entropy_inequalities,
        lem:chain_crossing_count}
    Choose two sites of $X$ with different membership in $D$, and order
    their coordinates as $a<b$. Along the integer interval from $a$ to
    $b$, the indicator of $D$ must change at two consecutive sites
    $p,p+1$. Thus $a\le p<p+1\le b<L$ and $p\in\partial D$.

    For every $x\in X$, the diameter bound gives
    $x\le a+R$ and $b\le x+R$. Hence
    \begin{align}
        \max\{0,p+1-R\}\le x<p+1+R,
    \end{align}
    proving $X\subseteq W_p$. Each window has at most $2R$ sites, so
    contains at most $2^{2R}$ subsets. Cover the split supports by the
    families of subsets of $W_p$, one family for each $p\in\partial D$,
    and bound the cardinality of the finite union by the sum of their
    cardinalities. This proves the stated bound, including the case of
    an empty boundary.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The boundary perturbation of a doubled chain]
    \label{thm:chain_partial_swap_boundary}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.partialSwap_term_difference_spec,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.partialSwap_difference_eq_sum_crossing,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.partialSwap_boundary_spec}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian,
        def:qc_site_pair_coordinates,def:peps_frame_sheet_swap,
        lem:chain_boundary_crossing_count}
    Let $L,q,R$ be natural numbers, let $J\in\mathbb R$, and let
    $H=\sum_Xh_X$ be a range-$R$ Hamiltonian on the contiguous chain
    $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$, with local dimension $q$ and
    $\|h_X\|\le J$. Let $D\subseteq I$ be arbitrary. In the paired
    coordinates $p$ of Definition~\ref{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian}, put
    \begin{align}
        F       & =p_*F_D,\notag                                \\
        T_{X,0} & =p_*(h_X\otimes\mathbb 1),\notag              \\
        T_{X,1} & =p_*(\mathbb 1\otimes h_X),\notag             \\
        K_{X,b} & =FT_{X,b}F^\dagger-T_{X,b}\quad(b\in\{0,1\}).
    \end{align}
    Every $K_{X,b}$ is Hermitian, is supported on the original set $X$,
    and satisfies $\|K_{X,b}\|\le2J$. Let $\mathcal C$ be the set of
    original supports meeting both $D$ and $I\setminus D$, and let
    $N_R=2\cdot2^{2R}$. For the doubled operator $\widehat H$,
    \begin{align}
        F\widehat H F^\dagger-\widehat H
                      & =\sum_{X\in\mathcal C}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}K_{X,b},\notag \\
        2|\mathcal C| & \le N_R|\partial D|.
    \end{align}
    Let $Z=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal C}X$ be the union of crossing
    supports and put $\Delta H=F\widehat H F^\dagger-\widehat H$.
    The operator $\Delta H$ is supported on $Z$. For every $z\in Z$ there
    is a boundary position $a\in\partial D$ with $|z-a|\le R$,
    and
    \begin{align}
        \|\Delta H\|\le 2J N_R|\partial D|.
    \end{align}
    These assertions include $q=0$, the empty chain, and empty or full
    $D$. They give the boundary decomposition on a contiguous chain used
    in the paragraph ``Two-sided Gaussian filtering'' of
    \cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}; the distinction from arbitrary finite
    induced domains is recorded
    in~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_doubled_hamiltonian,thm:qc_site_pair_tensor_support,
        thm:area_law_supported_identity,thm:peps_sheet_swap_conjugate_support,
        thm:peps_frame_sheet_swap_norm,
        thm:peps_sheet_swap_doubled_cancellation,
        lem:chain_boundary_crossing_count}
    The support reduction for a conjugated tensor product in paired
    coordinates shows that $FT_{X,b}F^\dagger$ is supported on $X$.
    The same is true of $T_{X,b}$, so subtraction preserves its support.
    Taking adjoints proves Hermiticity. Pairing coordinates preserves
    the operator norm, and the regional swap satisfies $\|F\|\le1$.
    Tensoring with an identity does not increase the operator norm, hence
    \begin{align}
        \|T_{X,b}\|           & \le\|h_X\|\le J,\notag \\
        \|FT_{X,b}F^\dagger\| & \le J,\notag           \\
        \|K_{X,b}\|           & \le J+J=2J.
    \end{align}

    Swapping exchanges the two replica terms of a support inside $D$ and
    fixes those of a support disjoint from $D$:
    \begin{align}
        FT_{X,0}F^\dagger & =T_{X,1},\quad FT_{X,1}F^\dagger=T_{X,0}
        \qquad(X\subseteq D),\notag                                  \\
        FT_{X,b}F^\dagger & =T_{X,b}\qquad(X\cap D=\varnothing).
    \end{align}
    Adding the two replica terms gives, for every support
    outside $\mathcal C$,
    \begin{align}
        F(T_{X,0}+T_{X,1})F^\dagger=T_{X,0}+T_{X,1}.
    \end{align}
    By definition, each doubled interaction is $T_{X,0}+T_{X,1}$.
    Summing these identities expresses $\widehat H$ as the sum of these
    pairs. Distributing conjugation and subtraction over the finite sum
    cancels each pair outside $\mathcal C$, leaving exactly the sum over
    the crossing supports. Finally, Lemma~\ref{lem:chain_boundary_crossing_count}
    gives $|\mathcal C|\le2^{2R}|\partial D|$; counting the two replica
    labels for each support gives the stated bound.

    Each term in the resulting expansion is supported on some
    $X\subseteq Z$, so their finite sum is supported on $Z$. If $z\in Z$,
    choose a crossing support $X$ containing it. The boundary-window
    inclusion gives $a+1-R\le z<a+1+R$ for a boundary position
    $a$, whence $|z-a|\le R$.
    If $L=0$, the boundary and the crossing family are empty and
    $\Delta H=0$. Otherwise, a singleton interaction implies
    $0\le\|h_{\{x\}}\|\le J$ at a site $x$, so $J\ge0$.
    The triangle inequality and the bound on the number of replica labels
    now give
    \begin{align}
        \|\Delta H\|\le 2J(2|\mathcal C|)\le 2J N_R|\partial D|.
    \end{align}
\end{proof}

% Source: TNLean2026OneDimensional, eq:ham; setting before lem:LR.
\begin{lemma}[Interaction norm budget at a site]
    \label{lem:chain_site_norm_budget}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.sum_norm_terms_containing_le,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.norm_bound_nonneg}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $L,q,R$ be natural numbers and $J\in\mathbb R$. On the chain of $L$
    sites with local dimension $q$, let $H=\sum_X h_X$ be a finite-range
    Hamiltonian with one term of norm at most $J$ for each nonempty support
    $X$ of diameter at most $R$. For every site $x$,
    \begin{align}
        \sum_{X\ni x}\lVert h_X\rVert\le 2^{2R+1}J.
    \end{align}
    The presence of a site also forces $J\ge0$.
    This is an auxiliary consequence of the Hamiltonian setting
    in Section~1 of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}; it supplies a
    per-site interaction estimate of the form assumed immediately before
    the Lieb--Robinson lemma there. No spectral-gap assumption is needed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model, lem:chain_crossing_count}
    Fix a site $x$. The singleton $\{x\}$ is an admissible support, so
    $0\le\lVert h_{\{x\}}\rVert\le J$. Every support containing $x$ lies in
    the clipped interval $[x-R,x+R+1)$, which has at most $2R+1$ sites.
    Counting subsets of this interval gives at most $2^{2R+1}$ such supports.
    Summing their norm bounds and using $J\ge0$ proves the claim. If the chain
    has no sites, the assertion is vacuous.
\end{proof}

\section{Locality on a contiguous chain}

\begin{theorem}[Lieb--Robinson estimates on a contiguous chain]
    \label{thm:chain_lieb_robinson}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.norm_heisenberg_commutator_le_chainDistance,
        TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.norm_heisenberg_sub_siteExpectation_chainNeighborhood_le}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model,def:qc_site_expectation}
    Let $L,q,R$ be natural numbers. Let $K$ be a finite set of interaction
    labels, and for each $k\in K$ let $X_k$ be a nonempty support of diameter
    at most $R$ in the contiguous chain $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$.
    Let $h_k$ be Hermitian operators supported on $X_k$, and suppose that
    $b_0\in\mathbb R$ satisfies
    \begin{align}
        \sum_{k:\,x\in X_k}\|h_k\|\le b_0
    \end{align}
    for every site $x$. Put $H=\sum_{k\in K}h_k$ and
    $\tau_t(A)=e^{itH}Ae^{-itH}$, and set
    \begin{align}
        C_0 & =2+4|b_0|e^{2R},\notag      \\
        v   & =2(R+1)|b_0|e^{2R}+1,\notag \\
        \mu & =\tfrac12,\qquad
        C'=\frac{2C_0e}{1-e^{-1/2}}.\notag
    \end{align}
    These constants are independent of $L,q$, the interaction family, and
    the observable support. For $S\subseteq I$ let
    $d(S,y)=\min_{x\in S}|x-y|$, with $d(\varnothing,y)=\infty$ and
    $e^{-\mu\infty}=0$. For every operator $A$ supported on $S$, every site
    $y$, every operator $B_y$ supported on $\{y\}$, and every real $t$,
    \begin{align}
        \|[\tau_t(A),B_y]\|
        \le C_0|S|\|A\|\|B_y\|e^{v|t|}e^{-d(S,y)/2}.
    \end{align}
    This estimate allows every natural local dimension $q$.
    If $q\ge1$, define, for every real $\ell\ge0$,
    $N_\ell(S)=\{y\in I:\exists x\in S,\ |x-y|\le\ell\}$.
    The normalized partial-trace expectation $E_{N_\ell(S)}$ then satisfies
    \begin{align}
        \|\tau_t(A)-E_{N_\ell(S)}(\tau_t(A))\|
        \le C'|S|^2\|A\|e^{v|t|-\ell/2}.
    \end{align}
    Both estimates allow $S=\varnothing$. This is the contiguous-chain case
    of the lemma ``Lieb--Robinson bound on a chain'' in Part~II
    of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}, as used for its Theorem~B;
    the distinction from the broader finite-domain reading is recorded
    in~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{lem:chain_path_metric_spheres,thm:area_law_supported_identity,
        thm:qc_graph_lieb_robinson_finset,
        thm:qc_graph_neighborhood_localization}
    Use the path graph on $I$. Its distance is $|x-y|$, each $X_k$ has
    graph diameter at most $R$ and at most $R+1$ sites, and its nonemptiness
    permits a choice of an anchor in $X_k$. The matrix-entry characterization
    of support supplies the hypotheses on the supported algebras.
    Apply the arbitrary-support graph commutator estimate with decay
    parameter $1$ and support-size bound $R+1$. If $S$ is nonempty,
    its least distance to $y$ is a finite nonnegative integer $d$, and
    $e^{-d}\le e^{-d/2}$. For $S=\varnothing$, the factor $|S|$ makes
    the commutator bound zero without selecting a point of $S$.

    For localization assume $q\ge1$ and put $m=\lfloor\ell\rfloor$.
    Every distance sphere of the path has at most two sites, so the graph
    localization estimate applies with $K_g=2$ and growth exponent zero.
    Since path distances are integers, the union of graph balls of radius
    $m$ about $S$ is exactly $N_\ell(S)$. The tail constant and the rounding
    bound are
    \begin{align}
        T_0      & =\frac{e^{1/2}}{1-e^{-1/2}},\notag \\
        e^{-m/2} & \le e^{1/2}e^{-\ell/2}.\notag
    \end{align}
    Thus the graph coefficient $2C_0T_0$, multiplied by the rounding factor
    $e^{1/2}$, is $C'$. Empty unions and the zero factor $|S|^2$ cover
    $S=\varnothing$ as well.
\end{proof}

\section{The buffered discount with a constant-width buffer}

\begin{theorem}[Localization of the relative-evolution generator]
    \label{thm:chain_relative_generator_localization}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.norm_relativeGenerator_sub_siteExpectation_le}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian,thm:chain_partial_swap_boundary,
        def:qc_site_expectation}
    Let $H$ be a range-$R$ Hamiltonian on the contiguous chain
    $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$ with local dimension $q\ge1$ and interaction
    bound $J\in\mathbb R$. Let $D\subseteq I$, and let
    $K=\widehat H$ and $K'=\widehat H'$ be its doubled and regionally
    swapped doubled operators. Write $\mathcal C_D$ for the original
    supports meeting both $D$ and its complement, and put
    $Z=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal C_D}X$.
    For every real $\ell\ge0$ and $t\in\mathbb R$, define
    \begin{align}
        B    & =N_\ell(Z),\notag         \\
        G(t) & =e^{itK'}(K'-K)e^{-itK'}.
    \end{align}
    Set
    \begin{align}
        b_0 & =2^{2R+1}(2J),\notag                          \\
        a   & =\frac{2(2+4|b_0|e^{2R})e}{1-e^{-1/2}},\notag \\
        v   & =2(R+1)|b_0|e^{2R}+1,\notag                   \\
        N_R & =2\cdot2^{2R}.
    \end{align}
    Then the normalized expectation on the paired physical sites satisfies
    \begin{align}
        \|G(t)-E_B(G(t))\|
        \le4|J|a(R+1)^2N_R L\,e^{v|t|-\ell/2}.
    \end{align}
    No nonemptiness of the chain or of $\mathcal C_D$, sign condition on
    $J$, or spectral-gap hypothesis is required. This is the intermediate
    generator estimate in the localization paragraph of the proof of the
    information-reset lemma in Part~II of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional},
    on a contiguous chain; the domain distinction is recorded
    in~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian,thm:chain_partial_swap_boundary,
        lem:chain_site_norm_budget,thm:chain_lieb_robinson,
        lem:qc_site_expectation_properties,thm:qc_site_expectation_supported_approximant}
    The swapped doubled interactions have range $R$ and bound $2J$.
    Their per-site norm sum is at most $b_0$. The chain localization
    estimate therefore has constants $a,v$ as displayed.
    By Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_partial_swap_boundary},
    \begin{align}
        K'-K            & =\sum_{X\in\mathcal C_D}\sum_{b\in\{0,1\}}A_{X,b},\notag \\
        \|A_{X,b}\|     & \le2J\le2|J|,\notag                                      \\
        2|\mathcal C_D| & \le N_R|\partial D|\le N_R L.
    \end{align}
    Each $A_{X,b}$ is supported on $X$, with $|X|\le R+1$.
    For one such term put $A=e^{itK'}A_{X,b}e^{-itK'}$ and
    $A_0=E_{N_\ell(X)}(A)$. The chain estimate gives
    \begin{align}
        \|A-A_0\|\le2|J|a(R+1)^2e^{v|t|-\ell/2}.
    \end{align}
    Since $X\subseteq Z$, the operator $A_0$ is supported on $B$ and is
    fixed by $E_B$. Hence
    \begin{align}
        A-E_B(A)     & =(A-A_0)-E_B(A-A_0),\notag \\
        \|A-E_B(A)\| & \le2\|A-A_0\|
        \le4|J|a(R+1)^2e^{v|t|-\ell/2}.
    \end{align}
    Conjugation and the expectation are linear. Summing this estimate
    over the crossing replica labels and using their cardinality
    bound proves the assertion. An empty crossing family gives the zero
    generator; the same nonnegative estimates include $J=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A supported Gaussian integral on a contiguous chain]
    \label{thm:chain_supported_gaussian_integral}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.exists_supported_gaussian_integral}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian,thm:chain_partial_swap_boundary,
        def:qc_site_expectation}
    For every natural number $R$ and real $J$, there are positive constants
    $C,v$, depending only on $R,J$, with the following property.
    Let $q\ge1$ and $L$ be natural numbers, let $H$ be a range-$R$
    Hamiltonian with interaction bound $J$ on the contiguous chain
    $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$, let $D\subseteq I$, and let $\ell\ge0$ be real.
    Put $K=\widehat H$, $K'=\widehat H'$ and
    $Z=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal C_D}X$, where $\mathcal C_D$ is the set of
    original supports crossing $D$. Write $N(S)=N_\ell(S)$.
    There is a differentiable unitary path $U$, supported on $N(Z)$ at
    every real time, such that
    \begin{align}
        U(0)  & =\mathbb 1,\notag                                  \\
        U'(t) & =iE_{N(Z)}\bigl(e^{itK'}(K'-K)e^{-itK'}\bigr)U(t).
    \end{align}
    The same path is chosen before all the following data.
    Let $Q\subseteq I$, let $W$ be any operator supported on $Q$,
    and let $\eta\ge0$ and $T\ge0$ be real.
    Let $\gamma_\eta$ be the centred Gaussian measure of variance $\eta$,
    with $\gamma_0$ the point mass at zero. Put $B=N(Q\cup Z)$ and
    define the operators
    \begin{align}
        M_{\mathrm{loc}} & =\int_{[-T,T]}
        E_{N(Q)}\bigl(e^{itK'}We^{-itK'}\bigr)U(t)\,d\gamma_\eta(t),\notag   \\
        M_T              & =\int_{[-T,T]}e^{itK'}We^{-itK}\,d\gamma_\eta(t).
    \end{align}
    Then $M_{\mathrm{loc}}$ is supported on $B$ and
    \begin{align}
        \|M_{\mathrm{loc}}\| & \le\|W\|,\notag                  \\
        \|M_{\mathrm{loc}}-M_T\|
                             & \le\|W\|C L^2(1+T)e^{vT-\ell/2}.
    \end{align}
    These assertions include the empty chain and empty supports.
    This is the localized integral in the localization paragraph of the
    information-reset proof in Part~II of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional},
    on a contiguous chain; the domain distinction is recorded
    in~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}\leanok
    \uses{def:chain_swapped_doubled_hamiltonian,
        thm:chain_relative_generator_localization,thm:chain_lieb_robinson,
        lem:chain_site_norm_budget,thm:area_law_supported_identity,
        thm:qc_supported_gaussian_projection_bounds}
    Let $a,v$ be the localization constants, put
    $d=4|J|a(R+1)^2N_R$ and $C=a+d>0$.
    Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:qc_supported_gaussian_projection_bounds} with
    $B_g=N(Z)$, $B_o=N(Q)$ and output region $B=N(Q\cup Z)$.
    The path is chosen from $K,K',N(Z)$ before $Q,W,\eta,T$.
    The physical generator and observable estimates, with $|Q|\le L$, give
    \begin{align}
        \varepsilon_g & =dLe^{vT-\ell/2},\notag   \\
        \varepsilon_o & =aL^2e^{vT-\ell/2}.\notag
    \end{align}
    Since $L\le L^2$ for every natural $L$ and $a,d,T\ge0$,
    \begin{align}
        aL^2+dLT & \le(a+d)L^2(1+T).\notag
    \end{align}
    Substitution in (\ref{eq:qc_gaussian_projection_error}) gives the stated
    error. The same theorem gives the support and norm bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Separation of the reset support from the cores]
    \label{lem:chain_reset_support_separation}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.chain_reset_support_separation}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$ be a contiguous chain, let $R$ be a
    natural number, and let $D,C,R_0,C_0\subseteq I$, with $D\cap C=\varnothing$.
    Suppose that a real number $w$ satisfies
    \begin{align}
        w & <|r-y| &  & (r\in R_0,\ y\in I\setminus D),\notag \\
        w & <|c-x| &  & (c\in C_0,\ x\in I\setminus C).
    \end{align}
    Put $Q=I\setminus(D\cup C)$. Let $\mathcal C_D$ consist of all
    nonempty supports $X\subseteq I$ of diameter at most $R$ that meet
    both $D$ and its complement, and put $Z=\bigcup_{X\in\mathcal C_D}X$.
    Then
    \begin{align}
        w-R & <|a-z| &  & (z\in Q\cup Z,\ a\in R_0\cup C_0).
    \end{align}
    Consequently, for every real $\ell$ with $\ell+R\le w$,
    \begin{align}
        \{y\in I:\ \exists z\in Q\cup Z,\ |z-y|\le\ell\}
        \cap(R_0\cup C_0)=\varnothing.
    \end{align}
    Empty chains and empty sets are allowed. No sign assumption on
    $w$ or $\ell$, or containment of the cores in the outer regions,
    is needed beyond the displayed hypotheses.
    This is the support-separation step in Part~II
    of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}, on a contiguous
    chain; see~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    If $z\in Q$, the two margin inequalities apply directly; weakening
    the lower bound from $w$ to $w-R$ is permitted since $R\ge0$.
    If $z\in Z$, choose a crossing support $X$ containing $z$,
    together with $y\in X\setminus D$ and $x\in X\cap D$.
    The diameter bound gives $|z-y|,|z-x|\le R$.
    For $r\in R_0$, the first margin and the triangle inequality give
    \begin{align}
        w<|r-y|\le|r-z|+R.
    \end{align}
    Since $D$ and $C$ are disjoint, $x\notin C$; the second margin
    gives the same conclusion for every $c\in C_0$.
    This proves the first assertion with the exact loss $R$.
    If a core site $a$ also belonged to the displayed neighborhood,
    there would be $z\in Q\cup Z$ with $|z-a|\le\ell$.
    Symmetry of distance would then give
    $w-R<|a-z|\le\ell\le w-R$, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Information reset on a contiguous chain]
    \label{thm:chain_information_reset}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.ChainHamiltonian.information_reset}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Fix a natural local dimension $q\ge1$, a natural range $R$, a real
    interaction bound $J$, a gap $\Delta>0$, and an exponent $p>0$.
    There are $C_p>0$ and a natural number $L_0\ge1$, depending only on
    these fixed parameters, with the following property.
    For every $L\ge L_0$, let $H$ be a range-$R$ Hamiltonian with
    interaction bound $J$ on the contiguous chain $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$.
    Let $\Omega$ be a normalized ground vector, with ground energy $E_0$
    and full-system gap at least $\Delta$.
    Let $D,C\subseteq I$ be disjoint, let $R_0\subseteq D$ and
    $C_0\subseteq C$, and let $b\in\mathbb R$ satisfy
    $I_\Omega(D:C)\le b$.
    Put $w=C_p(1+b+\log L)$ and suppose that
    \begin{align}
        w & <|x-y| &  & (x\in R_0,\ y\in I\setminus D),\notag \\
        w & <|x-y| &  & (x\in C_0,\ y\in I\setminus C).
    \end{align}
    Then
    \begin{align}
        I_\Omega(R_0:C_0)\le L^{-p}.
    \end{align}
    The constants $C_p,L_0$ precede the length, Hamiltonian, state,
    regions and information bound. Only the original full-system gap
    is assumed. This is the contiguous-chain form of the information-reset
    lemma in Part~II of~\cite{TNLean2026OneDimensional}; the domain distinction
    is recorded in~\cite{gap:tnlean_one_dimensional_connected_domain_scope}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{thm:peps_reset_regional_filter,thm:chain_supported_gaussian_integral,
        thm:peps_reset_polar_step,lem:chain_reset_support_separation,
        thm:regional_reset_cores_entropy,thm:peps_reset_entropy_scalar}
    Set $r=2p+10$. Choose the Gaussian constants $A,T_0>0$ from
    Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_reset_regional_filter} once, and choose the locality
    constants $C,v>0$ from Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_supported_gaussian_integral}.
    Put
    \begin{align}
        P                & =C(1+T_0),\notag       \\
        K_{\mathrm{loc}} & =4(vT_0+15+r+P),\notag \\
        C_p              & =K_{\mathrm{loc}}+R+4.
    \end{align}
    Choose a natural $L_0\ge\max\{1,8\sqrt8(1+\log q)\}$.
    All these choices precede the instance data.
    Nonnegativity of mutual information gives $b\ge0$. Hence, for
    $B_*=1+b+\log L$, one has $B_*\ge1$.

    Put $Q=I\setminus(D\cup C)$, let $Z$ be the union of the supports
    crossing $D$, and put $\Psi=\Omega\otimes\Omega$, $\Phi=F_D\Psi$.
    The regional Gaussian theorem constructs the buffer contraction
    $W$ and a real overlap $z\ge e^{-2b}$; its truncated two-sided integral
    $M_T$, of variance $AB_*$ and cutoff $T=T_0B_*$, satisfies
    \begin{align}
        \|M_T\Psi-z\Phi\|,\quad\|M_T^*\Phi-z\Psi\|
        \le3\varepsilon,\qquad
        \varepsilon=e^{-10b}L^{-r}.
    \end{align}
    Take $\ell=\lceil K_{\mathrm{loc}}B_*\rceil$.
    Theorem~\ref{thm:chain_supported_gaussian_integral} gives its supported
    approximation $M_{\mathrm{loc}}$ on
    $S=N_\ell(Q\cup Z)$.
    The elementary exponential estimate, reserving a factor
    $e^{-\ell/4}$ to absorb $P$, gives
    \begin{align}
        \|M_{\mathrm{loc}}-M_T\|
         & \le C L^2(1+T_0B_*)e^{vT_0B_*-\ell/2}
        \le\varepsilon.
    \end{align}
    Indeed $\ell/4\ge P$, $P e^{-P}\le1$, and the remaining quarter-decay
    absorbs $L^4(1+T_0B_*)$, with coefficient $P$; here $L^2\le L^4$.
    The two normalized ground vectors therefore have residuals at most
    $4\varepsilon$ for $M_{\mathrm{loc}}$.
    The same-support polar correction of
    Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_reset_polar_step} constructs a unitary $V$ on $S$
    satisfying
    \begin{align}
        \delta:=\|V\Psi-\Phi\|\le8L^{-r}.
    \end{align}

    The ceiling bound and $B_*\ge1$ give
    $\ell+R<C_pB_*=w$.
    Lemma~\ref{lem:chain_reset_support_separation} makes $S$ disjoint from
    both cores, so $V$ acts trivially on their joint marginal in each
    replica. The entropy comparison of
    Theorem~\ref{thm:regional_reset_cores_entropy}, applied to the unitary
    $V$ supported on $S$, yields
    \begin{align}
        I_\Omega(R_0:C_0)
         & \le8\sqrt\delta\,\log(e q^{|R_0|})\notag \\
         & \le8\sqrt\delta\,(1+L\log q).
    \end{align}
    Since $L\ge L_0$, Theorem~\ref{thm:peps_reset_entropy_scalar}, with
    $r=2p+10$, bounds the last expression by $L^{-p}$.
    Empty cores cause no exception: their mutual information is zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The size of a coordinate enlargement]
    \label{thm:chain_interval_enlargement_size}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.card_interval_enlargement_sdiff_le}
    \leanok
    Let $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$ and let $l,r,v$ be natural numbers.
    With truncated subtraction at the left endpoint,
    \begin{align}
        \bigl|([l-v,r+v)\cap I)\setminus(\lbrack l,r)\cap I)\bigr|
         & \le2v.\notag
    \end{align}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    The difference lies in $([l-v,l)\cap I)\cup(\lbrack r,r+v)\cap I)$.
    Each of these two intervals contains at most $v$ sites.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Buffered discount from a common shell budget]
    \label{thm:chain_family_budget_discount}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.two_mul_condEntropy_interval_le_of_family_budget}
    \leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_single_splitting,lem:chain_crossing_count}
    Fix natural numbers $q\ge1$, $R$ and $N$, real numbers $J\ge0$ and
    $\Delta>0$, and a real number $B\ge1$. Put $\vartheta=J/\Delta$ and
    $\ell_R=\log(e\,q^{R+1})$. Let $H=\sum_{i\in F}h_i$ act on a chain of any
    finite length $L$, with local spaces $\mathbb C^q$. Here $F$ is finite,
    each $h_i$ is Hermitian, has norm at most $J$, and is supported on a set
    $S_i$ of diameter at most $R$. Suppose that every interval
    $D=[u,v)\cap I$, where $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$, is split by at most $N$ of
    the sets $S_i$ and that
    \begin{align}
        1+\sum_{\substack{i\in F:\ S_i\cap D\ne\varnothing \\
                S_i\setminus D\ne\varnothing}}
        \log^2(e q^{|S_i|}) & \le B.\notag
    \end{align}
    Choose a natural number $m$ satisfying
    \begin{align}
        m & \ge\max\bigl\{4,\
        \min\{32Nq^{2(R+1)},\ 64\,e\,N\ell_R^2\}\vartheta,\
        128\sqrt{(1+\vartheta)B}\bigr\}.
        \notag
    \end{align}
    Let $\Omega$ be a unit vector satisfying $H\Omega=E_0\Omega$ and
    $H-E_0\ge\Delta(1-|\Omega\rangle\langle\Omega|)$. For natural endpoints
    $l,r$ put $X=[l,r)\cap I$ and $T=(\lbrack l-mR,r+mR)\cap I)\setminus X$, with
    truncated subtraction at the left endpoint. Then
    \begin{align}
        2\bigl(S_\Omega(X\cup T)-S_\Omega(T)\bigr)
         & \le S_\Omega(X)+\frac{4096\,e\,\vartheta B}{m}+m\log2.\notag
    \end{align}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
    \leanok
    \uses{thm:chain_single_splitting}
    This is the argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:chain_buffer_discount}, with the
    crossing count $N_R$ and the cut budget $B_R$ replaced by $N$ and $B$.
    Use the nested shells $X_j=[l-(j+1)R,r+(j+1)R)\cap I$, $0\le j<m$, and
    the uniform weights $a_j=2/m$. The diameter bound implies that each
    support is split by at most one shell. The energy of the filtered vector
    is at most $16Nq^{2(R+1)}J/m$ by the squared-dimension estimate and at
    most $32\,e\,N\ell_R^2J/m$ by the logarithmic estimate of
    \cite[Part~I, Remark~6.3]{TNLean2026OneDimensional}; the choice of $m$
    makes the smaller of the two bounds at most $\Delta/2$. The error terms
    sum to at most $4096e\vartheta B/m$, and the overlap losses to $m\log2$.
\end{proof}

% Source: part1_arealaw.tex, eq:CE, lem:buffer and eq:final,
% lines 366--409, 517--645 and 674--679.
\begin{theorem}[Buffered discount with the original interaction energy]
    \label{thm:chain_polynomial_buffer_discount}
    \lean{TNLean.MPS.AreaLaw.two_mul_condEntropy_interval_le_polynomial}
    \leanok
    \uses{def:chain_model}
    Let $\Omega$ be a unit gapped ground vector, with gap $\Delta>0$,
    of a range-$R$ chain Hamiltonian with local dimension $q\ge1$
    and term norms at most $J\ge0$. Put
    $\vartheta=J/\Delta$, $N_R=2\cdot2^{2R}$ and
    $B_R=1+N_R\log^2(e q^{R+1})$. Define
    \begin{align}
        C_E^{\mathrm{pol}} & =8N_RJq^{2(R+1)},\notag \\
        m_{\mathrm{pol}}   & =\Bigl\lceil\max\Bigl\{
        4,\frac{4C_E^{\mathrm{pol}}}{\Delta},
        128\sqrt{(1+\vartheta)B_R},
        4096e(1+\vartheta)B_R\Bigr\}\Bigr\rceil.
        \label{eq:chain_polynomial_shells}
    \end{align}
    For natural endpoints $l,r$, let $I=\{0,\ldots,L-1\}$ and put
    $X=[l,r)\cap I$ and
    $T=([l-m_{\mathrm{pol}}R,r+m_{\mathrm{pol}}R)\cap I)\setminus X$,
    with truncated subtraction at the left endpoint. Then
    \begin{align}
        2\bigl(S_\Omega(X\cup T)-S_\Omega(T)\bigr)
         & \le S_\Omega(X)+1+\frac{2C_E^{\mathrm{pol}}}{\Delta}.
        \label{eq:chain_polynomial_discount}
    \end{align}
